Intrusive Rocks of The Taokeian StageThey are referred to as the intrusive rocks formed in accompany withthe Jiaodong Movement and the Fenzishan Movement during the Archaeanto Proterozoic. The rocks intruded into the Jiaodong Group or the JiaonanGroup during the Jiaodong Movement belong to the early Taokeian stage;and those intruded into the Fenzishan Group or the Wulian Group belongto the late Taokeian stage.
(1) The intrusive masses of the Taokeian stage in W. Shandong havemainly intruded into the Taishan Group with intrusive contacts or havemigmatization-metasomatic contacts with it. In the masses there are manyrestites and xenoliths of the Taishan Group and other rock masses of theTaishanian stage. The masses are distributed commonly in NW or NNWstructures. The isotopic age data of the masses vary from 1,754 to 2,075Ma. The Hongdonggou metagabbro mass, the quartz diorites and plagioclasegranites of the Taokeian stage in Puzhaosi, Zhongtianmen and Xianyu-Houpeng are the main representatives.The Hongdonggou metagabbro mass was formed by three intrusion events; the average content of SiO₂ being 53.76%, the average acidity 3.59, theaverage basicity 16.3, the mafic index M/F = (Mg²⁺ + Fe²⁺)/(Fe²⁺ + Fe³⁺ +Mn²⁺) × 100% = 51.2, belonging to basites. Rittmann's index for the rockis 1.626, the average value of Na₂O/K₂O 2.96 with Na₂O higher than K₂O.It is distributed in Taishan and Yishan, with the major masses being those in Puzhaosi,Zhongtianmen and Xianyu-Houpeng. In the plot showing the percentage of atomic weight (Fig. II-3-4), 70% samples from the Puzhaosi mass and 91%samples from the Zhongtianmen mass fall into the granite field; on thecontrary, samples from the Xianyu-Houpeng mass fall outside the field ofmagmatic rocks, indicating that the former two masses comprise mostlyremelting granodiorites and the latter, perhaps metasomatic granodiorites.
(2) Intrusive rocks of the Taokeian stage in the north Jiaodongpeninsula are chiefly ultrabasic ones which are distributed in groups andzones in the areas of Sujiazhuang and Pozi in Zhaoyuan County, Malianzhuangin Laixi, Sikou-Tangjiapo in Qixia, Yangjiaogou in Weihai and Wanglianzhuang in Rongcheng County, generally along the axes of basement folds. 3. Intrusive Rocks of The Early Yanshanian StageThey were intruded into the Jurassic strata and formed before the earlyCretaceous epoch. The time for the intrusion activities lasted 40 Ma (from180 to 140 Ma), i.e. in the early Yanshanian stage which can be dividedinto two phases featured by the intrusive granites.The intrusive masses in the first phase include the Linglong, Cishan,Queshan, Kunyushan and Wendeng masses, all of which have similar geological features, petrogenetic series and metallogenetic controls. Among thesemasses the Linglong granite mass has been studied in more details.The Linglong granite mass is situated along the axis of the Qixia anticlinorium with its southwest part extending in NE-direction due to theinfluence of the NE-trending faults. The mass has intruded into the Jiaodong—or the Fenzishan Group. Its U-Pb isotope age being 160 Ma and K-Ar agebeing 191 to 140 Ma. The rock types in the mass include chiefly adamellite,quartz monzonite and granodiorite. In the mass, such minerals as kamacite andmeteoric iron of cosmic origin have been found and feldsparsenclosing sparry iron in the form of small nodular aggregates have been recognized. Theenrichment in δ³⁴S, the oxygen isotope values (δ¹⁸O), +7.4 to +10.43, theinitial ⁸⁷Sr/⁸⁶Sr ratios, 0.710—0.718. The trace element geochemistry of therocks has shown two characters: (1) the lower Rb content and the higherBa content, (2) the gold abundance is five times higher than the Clarkevalue. The value of ZREE is lower than the average one of granites in theworld, but HREE value has a tendency to be increased as compared tothe LREE value. Moreover, the high Eu/Eu* ratio is an obvious feature.The La/Sm-La diagram drawn according to Taylor's distribution coefficientprinciple is consistent with the model of partial melting for the petrogenesisof the Linglong mass.The intrusive rocks of the second phase include those of the Guojialingand Mengjia masses which have been studied in detail. Intrusive Rocks of The Late Yanshanian StageThey were intruded into the Cretaceous System and formed before theTertiary. In this stage there were polyphase intrusion events that formedintrusive rocks of polycyclic and polygenic, including those of the Aishan andLaoshan phases.
(1) Aishan phase in which the intrusive rocks intruded into the lowerCretaceous Qingshan Fm. and were later covered by the upper CretaceousWangshi Formation. This phase lasted for about 40 Ma (140—100 Ma).In Western Shandong the magmatic activity in the Aishan phase beganwith basic intrusion events, reached to the maximum with the formationof intermediate complex bodies and ended with the formation of intermediate to basic dykes (veins). They include the Jinan, Zouping, Laiwu complexesand Chishan alkaline complex. For example, the Jinan complex is a mainlyintermediate to basic one consisting of gabbros, diorites and syenites. It isdistributed in the Jinan-Guodian area and extends in E-W direction. Theearliest horizon intruded by the Jinan complex is the Carboniferous-PermianSystem while nine isotopic age values for the complex range from 276 to82.6 Ma. According to the field contact relationship, three intrusive eventscan be identified with the main body containing three lithological facies. In

〔564〕
the first event were formed gabbros, in the second, diorites, and in thethird, syenites. Either from the inner facies to the border facies of the firstintrusion or from the first intrusion to the third one, the An content ofplagioclases decreases gradually, showing an evolutionary trend from basic toacid, with the degree of order going up obviously and the crystallizationtemperature falling down gradually. In addition, the SiO₂ content of therocks goes up progressively while, on the contrary, the basicity and colourindex fall down gradually; the rocks show an evolutionary trend from basicto acid and from calcareous to calc-alkaline and alkali-calcareous, the degreeof Al enrichment (Al/(Na+K+1/2Ca)) decreases gradually; the chemical composition trend of the rocks evolves from the Mg-Fe²⁺-Fe³⁺- and Ca-richtypes to the Na- and K-rich ones. In addition, the special alkaline Chishancomplex, which is situated in the area between Tanghu of Weishan Countyand Shagou of Zaozhuang City, consists mainly of syenites, alkaline granitesand various kinds of veins; its main part is aegirine-augite-quartz syenite,a small stock stretching in NW-SE direction and dipping to southwest. Dueto alkali metasomatism, minerals, such as albite, acmite-augite, arfvedsonite,Na-rich pyrochlore, thorite and monazite are present in the rocks which,in addition, contain bastnaesite and high contents of rare earth elements ofcerium family. The rocks have the average SiO₂ content being 68.53% andthat of Fe₂O₃, FeO, CaO and MgO being less than 2%, and hence belong toacid rocks; as the average association index value (α = (Na₂O+K₂O)/(SiO₂-43)) indicates, therocks belong to the calc-alkaline rock series. Moreover, a small amount ofstandard mineral aegirine is present.In E. Shandong, distributed are mainly intermediate-acid and acid rockswhich occur as composite masses. The Aishan composite mass is a representative among them, which includes adamellite-granites in Mts. Aishan andZhuajishan. In the tuffaceous sandstone-c.

The alkali ratio A.R. =(Al₂O₃+CaO+Na₂O+K₂O)/(Al₂O₃+CaO-Na₂O-K₂O) ranges from 2.76 to 4.13, the differentiation index (DI = q+ab+or+ne) ranges from 78.80to 90.61, indicating that in the petrogenetic process the magmatic differentiation proceeded further. In one word, the Aishan and Zhuajishan massesare similar and related to each other in terms of mineralogy and chemicalcomposition, assuming they originated from the same magma chamber, i.e.adamellite magma chamber which was formed in the depth by remeltingand was similar to granodiorite in composition. The magma migrated upduring tectonic movements (mainly faulting), evolved (mainly by differentiation) continuously in the course of migration and solidified into masses ofdifferent composition—the Aishan paraporphyritic adamellite and the Zhuajishan dense-porphyritic adamellite in different time and different geologicalconditions.Intrusive rocks in the Yishu fault zone are best developed in the Tongjing area where they occur mainly as small intermediate and acid stockswith a few small intermediate to basic stocks, consisting chiefly of biotitegranodiorite, adamellite porphyry and norite-gabbro.
(2) Laoshan phase, to which the rocks having intruded into Mb. 2 andcovered by Mb. 3 of the Wangshi Formation of the upper Cretaceous areascribed. This phase lasted for 20 Ma (from 100 to 80 Ma), and the rocksformed are distributed chiefly in the Jiaonan uplift and Mt. Laoshan ofQingdao. They are predominantly acid and alkali-acid granites comprisingsingle or composite mass(es). The Laoshan mass (with isotopic age of 101—132 Ma) is the best representative. According to its lithologic feature andcrosscutting relationship among individual intrusions five intrusive eventshave been recognized in which a series of alkali-granites were formed. Inthe first event were formed intrusive rocks of mainly medium-coarse-grainedpotash feldspar granite, in the second event, medium-fine-grained.

The degree of Al-enrichment ranges from 1.36 to 1.44while the differentiation indices range from 93.97 to 96.01, so the rocksbelong to strongly fractionated acid granites.Moreover, between the Yiyuan fault zone and the Shangwujing fault inMengyin of W. Shandong, in an area with a length of about 55 km and awidth of about 18 km, there are three kimberlite zones (Changmazhuang +Xiyu and Poli), which perhaps were formed at the end of the Yanshanianstage or the early Eogene with isotopic age of 88 Ma. The rock types include chiefly fine-grained kimberlite, porphyritic (hypabyssal) kimberlite andkimberlite breccia. The amounts of main oxides of porphyritic kimberlite,including SiO₂, TiO₂, Al₂O₃, FeO, Fe₂O₃, P₂O₅, are similar to Daly's; theSiO₂/Mg ratio is near 1 which is generally similar to the chemical compositions of kimberlite in other provinces of the world, MgO content is lowerwhile CaO content is higher than Daly's. The chemical compositions of thefine-grained kimberlite vary widely because phenocrysts and accessoryminerals in the rock are not distributed evenly; the chemical compositions ofauto
chthonous fragments are more uniform.In short, magmatic rocks of the late Yanshanian stage can be dividedinto two classes of the Aishan and Laoshan phases, which are the productsof different phases in the same tectonic-magmatic cycle. They are differentbut are related to each other by progressive evolution which was a continuous and irreversible process.Considering the time when the Jinan complex was formed, the magmaticactivity of the Yanshanian stage in W. Shandong initiated from theHercynian stage, reached its peak in the Aishan phase of the lateYanshanian stage and died out quickly with a sudden change in style.While the intermediate to basic volcanic rocks erupted in the edges offaulted basins, many intermediate to basic intrusive complex bodies wereformed one after another as lopoliths, sills or stoc..

...by an ascending style, the magmatic activity reached the first peak in thisarea in the Aishan phase. With the development of fractures and faults themagmatic intrusion activity remained very intense in the Laoshan phaseand reached the second peak in the regional geological history which wasfeatured by a descending style and by forming large composite masses of(medium-coarse-grained) granite-potash granite-(fine-grained) graniteassociation as batholiths. The magmatic activity then gradually weakenedand finally died out.5. Intrusive Rocks of the Himalayan StageThey occur only on a small scale, chiefly as ultrabasic and basic dykesin Shandong. The ultrabasic dykes are distributed in the area of Xujialiugou, Xujiapandian of Fentuan in northeastern Juxian and belong to brecciatedalkali-picrite-porphyrite, intruding into porphyritic syenites of the late Yanshanian stage and trending in NWW direction. Petrochemically they belongto the alkali-ultrabasic rocks of potash-series and are similar to alkali-ultrabasic nephelinite of the Cenozoic volcanic rocks in Shandong. In addition,there are also some basic dykes distributed in W. Shandong, such as diabasedykes in Yiyuan.6. Mode of Formation and Source MaterialsMagmatic rocks in Shandong were manifold and polygenic in terms ofthe mode of formation and their source materials. They can be classifiedinto three genetic series: (1) mantle-derived series, (2) remelting geneticseries of mixed source materials which, according to the source materialsforming the rocks, can be further divided into two genetic subseries: (i)remelting genetic subseries of mixed source materials consisting mainly ofcontinental crust material with some mantle-derived material participatingin; (ii) syntexis genetic subseries of mixed source materials chiefly comprising of mantle material with some crust material participating in; (3)crust-transformed genetic series which, according to the contact relationship,i.e. whether the masses had moved or not, can be subdivided into (i) autochthonous granites of the crust-transformed types, (ii) parautochthonousgranites of the crust-transformed type.Magmatic rocks in Shandong Province are mostly those of the remeltinggenetic series of mixed source materials, especially predominated by the remelting genetic subseries of mixed source materials, secondly, of the crust-transformed genetic series. The magmatic rocks of the three genetic seriesevolved in parallel with each other, particularly the remelting genetic seriesand crust-transformed genetic series were often evolved contemporaneously,related with each other but developed independently.

〔568〕
7. MineralizationMagmatic rocks in Shandong were influenced by basement materials andhave obvious mineralization. The gold mineralization which is closely relatedto the granites occurring chiefly in the early Yanshanian stage, while Cu.the late Yanshanian stages iron mineralization is closely related to the inte-
rmediate to basic intrusions and also occurred mainly in the Yanshanianstage, other types of ores are closely conriected with the magmatic rocks ofthe late Yanshanian stage as well.PART III Metamorphic Rocks And Metamorphism1. Metamorphic Rock Types and Restoration of Its Original RocksThe metamorphic rocks in Shandong Province are very complex, whichare widely distributed in the pre-Cambrian terranes, and each group has itsOwn characteristic rock association.The green-schists in the Taishan Group are distributed mainly in Yanli--
Yanlingguan Formation occur in great thickness and in bands, most of themare green, greyish-green and greyish-white in colour, showing schistose stru-cture, and lepido-nematoblastic, nematoblastic, granoblastic and prismatobla-stic textures, their original rocks were mainly ultrabasic lavas and basicvolcanic rocks. Komatite has been found in the ultrabasic lavas and its ty-pical chemistry shows that Si0z= 46-53% (or greater than 45%), Al20g lessthan 10%, Mg0=10-20% (or more than 9%), K20 less than 0.9% and Ca0/AlzOs ratios equal to or lager thaln one, the An content of plagioclases pre-sent is higher than 30. Hornblendes are chiefly green with some being blui-shgreen in colour. The main rock types are chlorite-tremolite schist, chloriteschist, actinolite schist, tremolite schist, chlorite-actinolite schist, biotite-acti-rolite-schist, talc-chlorite-tremolite schist and serpentine-chiorite-tremolitescbist,etc.Armphibolic rocks which are exposed in the areas of Taipingding of Mt.Mengshan, Mt. Xinfushan, Yanlingguan of Xintai, and Wande of Jinan areof the Yanlingguan Formation, they include amphibolite, hornblende-plagio-clase gneiss, amphibole schist and hornbendite, generally the rocks show

Blastovesicular (wormtube-shaped), blastoamygdaloidal, blastodiabasic, blasto-tholeiitic and blastoporphyritic textures. Their protoliths belong to basiclava, pyroclastic rocks and basic intrusive rocks.Gneiss rocks are more common in the Minshan and Fuyang formationsof the Jiaodong Group, and include biotite-plagioclase gneiss, hornblende-plagioclase gneiss, hornblende-biotite-plagioclase gneiss, sillimanite-garnet-biotite-muscovite-plagioclase gneiss, sillimanite-bearing garnet-biotite-K-feldspar gneiss, garnet-bearing kyanite-biotite-plagioclase gneiss, the protoliths of aluminium-rich or graphite-bearing gneisses are mostly argillaceous rocks, thoseof hornblende-bearing biotite-plagioclase gneiss are chiefly quartz-andesite,andesite, andesite-basalt lavas and pyroclastic rocks.Granulites of the Jiaodong Group are mainly distributed in a belt fromLaixi, through Pingdu and Changyi, to Anqiu, which lies in the south flankof the Qixia anticlinorium. They include leucocratic granulite and hornblende-two pyroxene granulite, often associated with diopside-plagioclase amphibolite, diopside amphibolite and amphibolite, crystals of apatite and zircon inthe rocks are characteristically rounded, the crystal cores and edges arerounded or disappear with the ratio of length to width being 1:1. Someof the protoliths of the rocks may be, in part, basic volcanic rocks.Eclogites distributed in the Jiaonan Group have their own characteristicsand always occur in the Zhenjiagou Formation in a small size of several toover ten meters long (with the longest one less than one hundred meters),as stratiform-like, lenticular and orbicular bodies in shape. Sometimes eclogites are also distributed as veins in ultrabasic rocks, such as in augite peridotite, garnet-pyroxenite and pyroxenite with a gradational contact relationship, and also in ultrabasic rocks, such as augite-peridotite or olivine-pyroxenite with either abrupt or gradational (or intercalation) contact relationship.
The Fenzishan and Wulian groups consist mainly of marbles and, inaddition, of garnet-bearing sillimanite-biotite schist, sillimanite-biotite schist,lumped tourmaline-bearing sillimanite-biotite-quartz schist, andalusite schistand chloritized plagioclase-biotite schist, etc.The tourmaline-bearing sillimanite-biotite-quartz schist which showslepido-granoblastic texture and schistose structure occurs mainly as interlayers and is composed of quartz (30-35%), plagioclase (20-25%), biotite(25-30%), sillimanite (15-20%) with a small amount of tourmaline and raremagnetite and zircon; sillimanite occurs as prisms, hairy or sheaf aggregates,and makes up the main component of the lumps in the rocks; tourmaline, with its well-developed transverse fissures, occurs as anhedral prisms. This

〔570〕
schist is often changed into gneiss facies, suggesting that its protoliths maybe aluminium-rich clay rocks.Andalusite-biotite schist occurs mainly in the Haiyankou Formation inDazhuzishan of Wulian County, and is blackish to dark-grey in colour. Itshows porphyroblastic and lepidoblastic textures and schistose structure. Themineralogy of the rock includes biotite (40%), andalusite (30%), quartz (25%)and carbonaceous matter (1%). Andalusite occurs as porphyroblasts whilebiotite shows pleochroism: Ng-brownish-red, Np-yellowish. Under microscopethe rock shows blastopsammitic texture and zircon is rounded. On the petro-chemical diagrams the data points of the rock are located in the region ofsedimentary rocks, so its protoliths may be clay and argillo-arenaceous sediments.
The Penglai Group is composed mainly of pebble-bearing quartzite, quartzite, phyllite-slate, slate and marble-limestone, which exhibit well-developedbedding structure, blastopelitic and blastoaleuritic textures. They belong tolow-grade metamorphosed sedimentary rocks.2. Characteristics of Migmatization(i) The regional metamorphic rocks of the Archaean Taishan Grouphave generally been subjected to intensive migmatization, forming partialmigmatite, migmatite and migmatitic granite, etc. which are distributed inMts. Taishan-Zulaishan, Xinfushan-Wujianshan, Mengshan, Fenghuangshan,Nishan, Yishan and Lushan, occupying about 4/5 of the total basement area.Rocks with different degrees of migmatization show a zonal distributionand from center to outside, the migmatization intensity decreases gradually.In the migmatitic centres are dominantly distributed migmatitic granite andmigmatitic granite gneiss, which are replaced successively by nebulite, homogenic migmatite, striped migmatite and augen migmatite outwards and thenby weakly migmatized regional metamorphic rocks further outwards. Thezonation is parallel to the regional lineament, and each centre correspondsgenerally to the anticline axis of the basement. According to the distributionof the centres there are several migmatitic fields, such as the Nishan-Baiyan-, Mengshan-, Northern Feicheng-, Taishan-Zulaishan-Xinfushan-, Ma'anshan-Lushan-Haoshan- and Wengqan-Gongdanshan-Fulaishan migmatitic fields (Fig. Ⅲ-2-7).The matrices (palaeosomes) of migmatites are mainly biotite and hornblende rocks, whereas the veins (neosomes) are felsic, cryptomerous and granitic rocks. After migmatization, SiO2 content of the rocks increased, andNa2O and K2O contents also increased to some extent whereas componentssuch as Fe2O3, FeO, Al2O3, MnO, MgO, TiO2 and CaO have been taken away

〔571〕
to various extent. With intensive migmatization, the quantity of microclineand quartz in the rocks has gradually increased with microcline up to25--50%, and quartz moxe than 20% the An content of plagoclases decrea-sed from andesine (An= 30-45) to oligoclase (An=14-28): and the arnountof biotite decreased (abous 5-8%); plagioclase has been replaced bymicrocline and progressively by albite, while perthite and nyrnekite haveforrned, hornblende has been replaced by biotite, which, in turn, with Ti and Feremoved,could have been metasomatized and converted to sphene and magnetite.In the intensively migmatized rocks in the Nishan area, the restites of porp-hyro blastic and schlieren migmatites often occur. The schlieren migmatite hasbeen covered by migmatitic granite in the Taipingding Formation in Namia-oshan and Qingyunshan areas, which suggests that there were two stages ofmigtnatization. The analysis has indicated that the first stage of migmatiza-tion is featured by potassium metasomatism with great intensity ani widematism (locally prominent Na-metasomatism) which is restricted to the str--ucturally favourable positions-migmatization centers where the two stageshave been superimposed onto the rocks.
(2) The migmatization jn the Late Archaean-Early Proterozoic Jian-dong or Jiaonan Group has been less intensive and on a much sinaller scalerocks have often formed. The Dashangou Formation and part of the Zhenjia-gou Formation of the Jiaonin Group belong to migmatitic geniss or homo-genic migmatite. The charactcristic of mignuatites in the lower Fenzishansubgroup of Proterozoic is similar to that in the Jiaodong Group. The mig-matization in the upper Fenzishan subgroup has been weak which is repre-sented by injections of felsic-, quartz- and pegmatite veins while metasoma-tism has been very weak. The fact that the regional migmatization has notaffected the Late Proterozoic Penglai Group reflects a trend that the olderthe metamorphic strata are, the more intensive the migmatization is.3. The metamorphic temperatures and pressures wereestimated from the hornblende-plagioclase, hornblende-garnet and biotite-garnet mineral pairs inthe rocks, so they belong to the low amphibolite facies of medium-pressurefacies series.
(3) The metamorphic grades of the Jiaonan Group, from high to low,are as follows: low amphibolite facies, high greenschist facies, and low greenschist facies, which all belong to the medium-pressure series. The intensityof metamorphism for this group is closely related to the age of the strata.The rocks of low amphibolite facies crop out widely in the Qiaozishanarea of Rizhao, and are also distributed in Taolin (Zhucheng)-Poli (Jiaonan)area in the north, and in Yushan (north Linshu) in the south of the area. 573

〔574〕
The representative metamorphic strata are the Dashangou Formation, of whichthe upper part consists of mica-two-feldspar gneiss, hornblende-plagioclasegneiss with bands of leucogranulitite, muscovite-quartz schist and amphibolitewhile the lower part, of biotite-plagioclase gneiss, biotite-plagioclase granulitite intercalated with amphibolite- and marble lenticles. The study of itsmineral assemblage has shown that epidote is absent in the metamorphosedbasic rocks and plagioclases are oligoclase-andesine. The metamorphic pressure is estimated to be 6-6.5 kbar and temperature 525-580℃, which are withinthe range of low amphibolite facies of medium-pressure series.The rocks of high greenschist facies are distributed in the southernLinshu-Pingshang (Junan) area and the Taolin (Zhucheng)-Qibaoshan (Jiaonan) area. The typical metamorphic strata are those in the Zhenjiagou Formation, of which the lower part consists of two-feldspar gneiss, muscovite-two-feldspar gneiss with leucogranulitite bands,while the upper part comprises biotite schists. As for the mineral assemblagein these rocks, hornblende has replaced actinolite and plagioclase belongs tooligoclase; in addition, there are some associated minerals, such as epidoteand garnet. The metamorphic pressures and temperatures are estimated to be3-6 kbar and 500-570℃, respectively, belonging to the range of high greenschist facies of medium-pressure series.The rocks of lower greenschist facies are those in the QiuguanzhuangFormation and the Yujialing Formation. The former is chiefly composed ofbiotite granulitite and leucogranulitite intercalated with biotite schist andactinolite schist; the latter consists mainly of biotite schist, actinolite schistand mica granulite with bands of marble and quartzite. In terms of mineralassemblage, amphiboles present are mainly tremolite and actinolite, which areassociated with albite and epidote. The metamorphic pressures and temperatures are estimated to be 3-6 kbar and 350-500℃, respectively, belongingto the range of low greenschist facies of medium-pressure facies series.

(5) The Wulian Group belongs to the amphibolite facies of medium-to low-pressure facies series and crops out on the north side of the Haoguanzhuang-Puluogu fault; this group can be approximately correlated withthe upper subgroup of the Fenzishan Group.
(6) The Penglai Group is mainly distributed in the areas of Qixia andPenglai and the Miaodao islands. The metamorphic rock association comprisesquartzites, slates, marbles and limestones, etc. According to the mere occurrence of sericite and chlorite in the metapelites, it can be classified as sericite-chlorite zone. Moreover, it belongs to the monofacies, i.e., the low green-

schist facies, without prograde metamorphic zoning.PART IV Tectonics1. Latitudinal Structural SystemAround latitude 34°20′—35°13′N, the north branch of Qinling latitudinalstructural system is developed as a belt from Heze-Tengzhou-Zaozhuang, across the Yishu fault zone, to Zhubian-Pingshang-Ganyu, which consists mainlyof the Fengpei convex, the Jinteng concave, the Taozao basin and a groupof nearly EW-trending folds and faults, mainly including the Heze-, Fushan-, Yutai-, Chanxian-, Taozao-, Xiangdi-Dafangqian and Nanshihe-Xidouling faults, with associated N-S-trending extension faults, such as the Jiaxiang-, Sunshidian- and Yishan faults.The latitudinal structural system was active as early as pre-Sinian time, but then attenuated during the Palaeozoic; it became violent again in theMeso-Cenozoic.2. Regional EW-Trending Structural BeltsTo the north of the latitudinal structural system mentioned above, several
interval of 30′—35′ (about 60—65 km), including (from north to south):
(1) the Penglai-Huangxian belt,
(2) the Qixia belt,
(3) the Pingdu-Jingangkou-Shidao belt,
(4) the Laiwu-Yiyuan-Jiaozhou belt,
(5) the Wenshang-Sishui belt.Except the Qixia belt, all of them are distributed discretely, scatteredand developed weakly in Shandong Province. The Qixia regional EW-trendingstructural belt has been very active and has had its basic form since pre-Sinian time, whereas the others were formed during the Meso-Cenozoicmainly by faulting and associated deposition.3. Regional NS-Trending Structural BeltsThe NS-trending structural belts are not well developed in the province. Most of them occur as tectonic slices, or locally, as discontinuous belts,including:
(1) the Tengzhoudianzi-Shanting-Guanqiao fault belt,
(2) the Yuwangshan-Cangshan structural belt,
(3) the Laomuzhuhe fault belt in Eastern Shandong.

〔581〕
The regional NS-trending structures occur mainly as faults, some ofthem were filled with the Meso-Cenozoic veins. They didn't come into beinguntil the late Mesozoic, so the NS-trending belts belong to the weakeststructural system in the area.4. Neocathaysian SystemThe Neocathaysian System is the dominant one that is best developed inthe area. The Yishu fault zone is a typical example. Two groups of faults(shear planes) associated with the Neocathaysian System are also well developed, which include those of the Taishan pattern, such as the Tai'an-Daw
angzhuang fault (embraced by a brush structure in W. Shandong), the Xiawei-, Shanxiangjia-Haoguanzhuang-, Xiaoguan-Fengcheng- and Daguangfu-Fujia faults, and of the Dayishan pattern including the Yidu-, Jiushan-,Hanwang-, Jinxingtou-, Wenzu- and Changqing faults, etc.The Neocathaysian NNE-principal-compressive-plane is accompanied by two groups of the NNW-trending (Dayishan pattern) and NE-trending (Taishan pattern) faults, which, in the province, emerged in the Jurassic, underwent many movements and finally were shaped in the late Eogene. The Neocathaysian System has been formed by the Meso-Cenozoic movements in the province. Its dominant part, the Yishu fault zone, had experienced a continuous left-handed compresso-shear movement for a long time,but then was transformed to a rift due to the left-handed extension whichresulted from the upwelling of upper mantle materials as the faultsdeeply cut through the crust and reached to the mantle in the Cretaceous.The fault zone underwent a violent compresso-shear movementagain in the Eogene, which lasted until the Quaternary. Therefore, the Yishu fault zoneis not only an important component of the Tanlu fault zone, but also a great fault zone in Eastern China.It begins from Tancheng, through Central Shandong, approximately following the Yihe, Shuhe and Weihe rivers and then goes into the Bohai Sea with a length of 330 km. Its south and north ends are covered bythe Quaternar
y sediments.
As a typical representative of the Neocathaysian System, the Yishu faultzone continued its left-handed rhaegmageny for a long time, which produceda series of associated structures resulting from rotation and shearing, suchas the Northern Shandong brush structure, the Western Shandong brushstructure and the "S"-shaped structure in northwest Jiaodong peninsula.
The fault zone outcrops discontinuously and extends for 250 km in NNEdirection (10°—25′) with the average strike of 17°; it is about 60 km wide 577

〔578〕
in the north but only 20 km in the south, and has a clear outline on theLANDSAT image (Fig. IV-1-8).The Yishu fault zone consists primarily of four major faults, i.e., theTangwu-Gegou-, the Yishui-Tangtou-, the Anqiu-Juxian- and the Changyi-Dadian faults, which in turn, from west to east, form a pattern of two grabens intervened by a horst in the middle, with the north and south endsbounded by the Meso-Cenozoic depressions. Folds are rare in the fault zone,but some open folds are developed in the Mesozoic depressions init. The major faults in the Yishu fault zone have been often intersected byEW-, NW- and NE-striking faults, so that some of them occur as brokenlines in plan view, which also reflects a feature of strong movements sincethe Mesozoic and has had a control over the deposition of the Mesozoicstrata and magmatic activity. Moreover, many secondary faults making upthe major ones are complex in nature, including normal-, reversed-, thrust-and transverse faults. The step normal faults (Babaozhuang), the imbricatedthrust faults (local assemblage in Wangjiamiao, Shaohuyu) or the normaland reversed faults (Shijing) can often be seen in the same section. Thecompressive normal faults (Kuaibaozhuang, Wanjiamiao) are unique in theYishu fault zone.The common occurrence of fault slices between some secondary faults isanother diagnostic feature of the Yishu fault zone. The slices may consistof the Taishan Group, the Tumen Group, the Palaeozoic or the Meso-Cenozoic sediments, such as the Meso-Cenozoic slices between the faults F1 andF2 (Jishan, Wangjiazhuangzi).The same major fault may occur in different style in different areas.For example, the fault F1 occurs, in turn, from north to south as a crumpled zone (Zhenwumiao), a sub-buried fault bunch (Wangjiamiaozi, Shijing), and the exposed single major fault (Yishuhe), which suggests that thedisplacement along the fault F1 has been gradually increasing from north tosouth since the Cretaceous.The fault zone has controlled the intrusion (Shijing) and eruption (Shaohuyu, etc.) of magmas, especially of basic, ultrabasic magmas and xenoliths thro
ugh the crust and reaches the upper mantle.The fault zone has experienced intensive neotectonic movements andhence is an important earthquake-controlling fault in Eastern China (asshown in Shijing, Jishan and Jizhuang).It is very likely that beneath the Yishu fault zone is an upwarped

〔579〕
Aeromagnetic data have shown that the depth of Mohorovicic discontinuityin the west of the Tangwu-Gegou fault is about 35-36 km (Fig. IV-1-10);it is only 25 km in the region between the Tangwu-Gegou and Yishui-Tangtou faults and is no less than 36 km in the Eastern Shandong block to theeast of the Changyi-Dadian fault. According to the results of magnetotelluric sounding in the Linyi area, with the Changyi-Dadian fault as a boundary, the electric conductive layer of the upper mantle beneath the EasternShandong block (to the east of the Yishu fault zone) lies at a depth below300 km, whereas that beneath the region to the west of the fault zone at a depth of onlyover 70 km; this also indicates that the Changyi-Dadian fault isa great deep fault reaching the mantle (Fig. IV-1-12).As the Yishu fault zone is an important fault zone in Eastern China, itis of great significance to learn its formation and development; therefore, many geologists have made investigations on it, but have had various ideasfor
a long time. Some believed that the fault zone could have formed as early as in the Archaeanor the Proterozoic and experienced mainly vertical movements which controlled the strata developed in Eastern and Western Shandong. Others believed that it experiencedleft-handed strike-slip movements. However, there is still a dispute on thequestion as to how much the strike-slip displacement could be. At first, basedon the aeromagnetic survey data, someone assumed the displacement to be100 km; later, according to the offsets of geologic bodies on the oppositesides of the fault zone, others suggested the distance ranging from 200 kmto 740 km. Although ideas were proposed one after another, so far none hasbeen accepted unanimously.5. North Shandong Brush StructureIt is considered that the W. Shandong upwarped region is the backboneof the North Shandong brush structure. The vortical layers from outer toinner are, in turn, the Huanghua depression, the Chengning rise and theJiyang depression;
a part of the Chengning rise lies in Shandong Province.The Huanghua and Jiyang depressions were mainly formed in the Eogene period when the oil source rocks deposited there. There are a series ofsecondary en echelon rises which, like the Chengning rise, are made up of; the
depressions become younger in age gradually from south to north, as well asthe oil source rocks, that is, there was a migration of deposition centres in

〔580〕
the vortical layers of this brush structure away from the backbone.This is a buried giant brush structure covered completely by the Neogene and Quaternary sediments. It is composed of three compressional vortical layers produced by the counter-clockwise rotation of the Western Shandong block in the Eogene due to the left-handed wrench movement of theYishu fault zone.6. West Shandong Brush (Vortex) StructureThe West Shandong vortex structure is developed in the Western Shandong upwarped region and is well exposed. Its backbone is the Nishan dome, while its vortical layers are a number of depressions and rises bounded bysome faults. The major faults include the Tai'an-Dawangzhuang-, Tongyedian-Sunzu-, Lianhuashan-, Xintai-Duozhuang-, Wensi- and Mengshan faults. The rises, depressions and faults occur in alternations, which shows afeature that the Meso-Cenozoic depressions are bounded in the north byolder r
ocks toward the south . The vortex structure is a clear arc projecting northward and converging southeastward.On the rises are distributed the Taishan Group and a little Palaeozoicrocks, while in the depressions occur the Meso-Cenozoic sediments of severalthousand meters thick.The vor
tex structure is closely related to the long-term left-handed wrench movements of the Yishu fault zone with a complexhistory.
7. "S"-shaped Structure in Northwestern Jiaodong PeninsulaIt lies in the Laizhou-Zhaoyuan-Qixia area and includes principally theSanshandao-, Huangxian-Laizhou-, Zhaoyuan-Pingdu- and Xilin-Douya faults. Except for the Sanshandao fault which is mostly covered by the Quaternary sediments, all faults are well exposed, making up a prominent "S"-shaped structure.The faults show distinct compresso-shear nature and are accompanied bysmall brush structures nearby, such as the Linglong brush structure.In the province, several well-known large to ultra-large gold depositsare
hosted just in the major and derived secondary faults.In the early stage of the Yanshanian movement, the Yishu fault zonestructure which controlled the intrusion of magmas and the formation of goldore deposits .8. Cathaysian Structural System

〔581〕
The Cathaysian structural system occurs mainly in the Moupin-Jimo line.which runs through the centre of the Jiaodong peninsula, and consists ofthe Taoling-Doushan-, Guocheng-Jimo-, Zhuwu-Dianji-, Haiyang-Qingdaoand Rushan-Liugezhuang faults.The fault zone exhibits clear compressional aad compreso-shear featureand is characterised by left-handed wrench movement with the maximumdisplacement up to 30 kmi. The taults appear to occur at more or less equalinterval of 10--12 km.This structural system was probably developed following the older NE-trending structure and had its activity in the Jurassic. However, the mosthave occurred in the late Yanshanian Stage or the late Cretaceous and waslikely to have lasted until the Eogene. Its movement might be related to theactivities of the Yishu fault zone.9. * 人"-type Structure in Eastern ShandongIt accurs in the Jiaobei, Jiaonan uplifts and the Jiaolai depression,and is convex northwestward to some extent, spreading as an arc in Jiaodongpeninsula.The Jiaolai depression is narrower in its north portion and becomeswider southwestward, forming an open "L"- shaped depressiondivergingsouthwestward. It neighbours with the Changyi-Dadian fault and the sedi-mentary basins in the depression are connected with each other. The Meso-zoic sedimentary-lithofacies distribution in the Jiaolai depression suggeststhat the sedimentary' center kept inigrating from southeast to northwestwith the sedinments overlapping the Jiaobei uplift.The “人"-type structure was active during the late Jurrassic to the lateCretaceous, or even to the Eogene. The faulting and magmatism took placemainly on the uplifts whereas the late Jurassic-Cretaceous volcanism andnormal sedimentation occurred chiefly in the depression.10. Basement StructureThe basement structure is probably a giant "drag arc'.The “Drag arc" pattern;the left-handed strike-slip of the Yishu faultin pre-Sinian time dragged the Nw-trending Taishan Group and the nearEW-trending Jiaodong and Fenzishan groups, respectively, resulting in anwu-Gegou fault was developed at the apex of the ancient dxag arc in W.Shandoag that changed the strike of the Taishan Group in the Yishu faltzone into the NE and NNE directions.

〔582〕
PART V Geological Evolutionary HistoryDuring the long history of geological development Shandong has expe.rienced many strong tectonic movements and yarious geological processes.With its own characteristics, Shandong shared the same unit with the North:China platform. In the monograph we intend to discuss the geologicalevolutionary history of Shandong since the Mesozoic era in terms of thetectonic (structural) systems. As the palaeo-structures prior to the Mesozoicare difficult ta restore, the pre-Mesozoic geologic evolution of Shandong is:analyzed and synthesized based on the geological-historical records, as.reflected fron the sedimentation, magmatism, metamorphism and tectonismin Shandong, and on the isotopic chronologic data., The geological evolutionhistory is described successively with respect to the Archaean-Proterozoictime, the Palaeozoic, the Mesozoic and the Cenozoic eras.1. Archaean-Proterozoic EraChangyi-Dadian fault of the Yishu fault zone, a series of geological events.with great difference have taken place. They are listed in Table V-l.Up to ncw,there is still a dispute over when the Changyi-Dadianwith continous activity and its age could dated back to the Archaean orthe Proterozoic. Featured by the vertical movements, the Western andEastern Shandong blocks experienced, in turn, subsidence and uplifting,receiving deposition or being eroded. As a result, the Archaean TaishanGrouP, the lower Proterozoic Jining Group and the Upper Proterozoic Tumen.Group of the Qingbaikou System have been developed in W. Shandong,whereas the Archaean-Proterozoic Jiaodong Group, the Lower ProterozoicFenzishan Granup or Wulian Group and the upper Proterozoic Penglai GroupPdeveloped in E. Shandong. Others believed that it was a Mesozoic faultwhich has had strong movements since the Mesozoic era, and has been aleft-handed wrench fault zone with a displacement of 100 km, or 200 km,or even740 in different opinions, It is likely that the fault may be a collision boun-dar
y between the two different geological blocks, for which the evolutionaryhistory of Western and Eastern Shandong will be discussed separately.
(1) Western Shandong RegionOn the supracrustal rocks in W. Shandong deposited the Taishan Group

〔583〕
tions consist of argillo-arenaceous and volcanic rocks, whereas the YanlingguanFormation—basic volcanic rocks, and the Shancaoyu Formation—argillo-arenaceous rocks. By the end of the Shancaoyu deposition period, the Taishan Group underwent amphibolite-granulite subfacies regional metamorphism in the Xintai stag
e,
which was associated with the regional migmatization, forming the Taishan-,Yishan-, Lushan-, Mengshan- and Nishan migmatite domes. Most of the Taishan Group was converted into migmatitic granite, homogeneous migmatite andpartial migmatite. With the termination of the Fuping Movement, the basement of Western Shandong uplifted and underwent erosion continuously,resulting in the absence of the strata prior to the Jining Group or thoseequivalent to the Wutai of the Jiaodong Group. During this uplift and erosion theregion underwent the following events: in the first event, under the action of NE stress, were formed the Cishanrock bodies; in the second event, under the intensive NE-NNE stress field, were induced the NW-trending thrusts along which developed the Zhongtianmen intermediate to acid intrusions.During the early time of the Early Proterozoic era, an aulacogen appearedin the west margin of the Taishan continental nucleus. It developed furtheras a linear trench and received argillaceous, argillo-arenaceous deposits andintermediate volcanics—the Jining Group, which, then, was subjected togreenschist-facies regional metamorphism, followed by the Taokeian magmatic intrusions. The crust was uplifted and subjected to erosion and planation,resulting in the absence of sedimentation in the late Early Proterozoic-Middle Proterozoic period. In the early Late Proterozoic, under the further NScompression, the Taishan continental nucleus was subjected to the longitudinal tension while the Yishu strait formed, which was gradually connectedwith the Qingbaikou sea spreading in Jiangsu and Anhui regions, and thenthe Tumen Group deposited.In the early Sinian time, as the Yishu strait rose, there was no sedimentation there, as well as in the W. Shandong upwarped area, from the Sinianto pre-Wushan formation time of the Cambrian.
(2) Eastern Shandong RegionAfter the termination of the Fuping movement, i.e., in the late Archaean-Proterozoic, the sedimentation proceeded continuously in the E. Shandongregion, which formed basic, intermediate to basic, intermediate to acid volcanicsaccompanied with arenaceous, argillo-arenaceous and carbonate sediments.The south and north subregions of the Jiaodong peninsula will be discussed

separately because the thickness of litho-facies in the south and north ofthe Jiaodong peninsula varies greatly, and the protolith formations arevery different.In South Jiaodong, the Dashangou Formation of the early Jiaonan Stage(lower Jiaonan Subgroup) was composed of intermediate to basic and basicvolcanic rocks—medium clastic formation; the Zhenjiagou Formation—intermediate to acid volcanic rocks—medium clastic formation. The basicvolcanic rocks mentioned above are generally equivalent to the oceanicbasalt, whereas the intermediate to acid rocks, to the island-arc andesite ofbathyal-abyssal facies along the continental margin. In the late JiaonanStage (upper Jiaonan Subgroup), the sedimentary environment was greatlydifferent from that of the early stage. The Qiuguanzhuang Formation wascomposed of intermediate to acid volcanic rocks (intercalated with intermediate
carbonates)—argillaceous to fine clastic formation . The depositional environment was featured by frequent vibration of the crust with the dramatic
variation in thickness of lithofacies, so it may belong to volcano-tectonicbasin and shallow sea environment.In North Jiaodong, the Jiaodong Group is considerably different fromthe Jiaonan Group. The Pengkuang Formation of the Jiaodong Group consistsof basic-intermediate to basic volcanics and sedimentary rocks with only alittle change in lithofacies thickness, and carbonates occur as lenses. Thissuggests that a large area in North Shandong was initially subsiding, formingan epeiric sea-bathyal environment, and later, the crustal differential movementbecame enhanced. The Minshan Formation is a suite of clay, clayey to carbonate sediments with basic volcanic rock bands. The Fuyang Formation belongsto clastic-intermediate to basic volcanics formation with the thickness ofthe beds varying substantially, and several different lithological beds occur in thesame horizon, which change gradually to one another along the strike ordip, suggesting there was a volcano-tectonic basin, or gulf, or lagoon environment.
Although the Jiaodong and Jiaonan groups show significant lithofacieschanges, they have similarities in terms of sedimentary and tectonic environment.
With the increase in thickness of sedimentary pile and in temperature,the sediments underwent regional greenschist-amphibolite-facies metamorphism.As in a few anticlines and faults the heat flow went upward successively, the migmatization became stronger and the homogeneous migmatite on the

〔587〕
Baowuding anticline thus formed. Under the NS compressional stress, theEW-
trending (or latitudinal) structures accompanied with some intrusions, suchas the Suoluoshu ultrabasic bodies of the early Taokeian Stage and theYuejishan granites, which were unconformably overlain by the FenzishanGroup. This is named as the Jiaodong movement.In the Early Proterozoic, the active crust was changed gradually into astable one in the region where the Fenzishan Group deposited as clasolite,turned to be a new extensional environment in which rift, graben andhorst formed in early time of the Early Proterozoic, then followed by the development of trenches where the Fenzishan and Wulian groups deposited.By the end of the Fenzishan Stage the Fenzishan and Wulian groupsunderwent medium to high grade regional metamorphism, followed by upwarping, being subjected to, at least twice, the N-S compressions and yieldingthe tight overturned folds in the lower Fenzishan Subgroup and the openfolds in the upper subgroup in EW-trending. The Fenzishan Group hasbeen overlain by the Penglai Group by unconformity, which stands for theFenzishan movement, whereas the Middle Proterozoic and the QingbaikouSystem of the Upper Proterozoic are absent in the region.During the Late Proterozoic (the Sinian), the area in the north of thePenglai-Fushan line started to subside and received deposition of the Penglai Group which was an ingressional series of sandstone-mudstone-carbonate,having been metamorphosed into quartzite, slate and crystalline limestone.After the Penglai Group had risen, a series of EW-trending open foldsformed, which occurred as monoclines in places and were inherited from the[……脱字]。The regionwas under a long-term uplifting and eroding state until the Mesozoic whenit began to receive new sediments.2. Palaeozoic EraThe Eastern and Western Shandong regions were in remarkably differentenvironments in the Palaeozoic. The E. Shandong underwent continuouserosion, while in the W. Shandong formed a series of marine strata consistingmainly of carbonates in the Early Palaeozoic and a set of coal measures.
This indicates that the Western Shandong region was a platform featuredby oscillatory movements and devoid of magmatic rocks at that time.3. Mesozoic EraAs Shandong is situated between the second uplift zone and the second

〔588〕
depression zone of the Neocathaysian System, the sedimentary formationsrmagmatic and tectonic activities occurring there during the Mesozoic weremore complicated than in the Palaeozoic and had very unique features.Moreover, the growth and development of the Yishu fault zone (an impo-rtant Neocathaysian member) has had an important control over the deve-lopment of a series of structures, magmatism, sedimentay formations in Sha--ndong.
(I) In the Triassic, the Neocathaysian Liaokao fault moved first, theeast block to the fault continued uplifting and erosion, so there was no.Triasic sedimentation there, whercas in the Dongming-Linqing depression.in the west of the fault deposited the Triasic Liaocheng Formation.
(2) In the early to middleJurassic, the Neocathaysian Yishu andLiaokao faults were undergoing continuously the left-handed strik-slipmovement, As a result, a series of NNE-trending depressios and uplifts.formed in Dongming-Linqing faulted basin while the NEE-trending basins:(Zibo and Fangzi) formed in the north margin of the W, Shandong upliftedarea, where deposited are the important coal measures (e. g. the FangziFormation) and the Santai Formation.The Yishu fault zone continued itsuplifting and left-handed shearing that changed the early, near NS-trending.Yisu fault zone into an important Neocathaysian fault zoe. In the E.Shandong uplifted area, only the Neocathaysian faults developed but neitherNeocathaysian basins nor sediments have ever been found yet.
(3） In the late Jurassic, the Neocathaysian System was more activeand the Yishu fault zone continued its left-handed shearing on the uprising.background that Produced the W. Shandong brush stucture system and somenew sags in which deposited the Fenshuiling Formation. To the east of the.
and Jiaobei uplifts and the Jiaolai depression in which deposited theLaiyang Fornation. Because the warm and moist climate was suitable forliving things to grow, plants, such as Psilophyta of Goniferae were mostflourishing at that times while Vertebrata, such as Pisces, evolved to itshigher stage. At the end of the late Jurassic, the Linglong. Kunyushan andQueshan granite masses emplaced along the Neocathaysian faults.( 4) In the Cretaceous the Yishu fault zone was still active, undergo-ing left-handed tenso-shearing movement in which developed the Anqiu-Juxian and Yishui-Tangtou faults bounding the Mazhan-Sucun and Anqiu-Juxian grabens, the"人"-type strucure system in E, Shandong and the brushstructure system in W. Shandong developed further on the original backgro-und that controlled the deposition of the Qingshan and Wangshi formations.

〔593〕
During the Qingshan period, there were three volcanic eruptive cycles withcomplex styles, including either central eruptions or fissure eruptionss andfrom south to north and from west to east. In addition, sub-alkaline voica-aic rocks occurred in some places.The intrusive activity in W. Shandong was relatively weak which formedmainly small rock bodies of calc-alkaline and ultra-alkaline rock series. On.the contrary, the intrusive rocks in E. Shandong were most developed,including mainly those granites formed in the Aishan phase, with which.some important onferrous metal ores were assocaited.In the Wangshi period, mainly red sandstones and conglomerates of.fluviolacustrine facies with well-preserved fossils, such as Tanins, Helopuszdanskyi were deposited.By the end of Cretaceous, this region was extensively uplifted andsubjected to erosion that led to the folding and inclination of local bedsaccompanied with magmatic intrusions of the Laoshan phase and sometimeshydrothermal polymetallic ore fillings (veins) occurred.4. Cenozoic Era
(1) In the Eogene, the Neocathaysian Yishu fault zone kept its left-handed compresso-shear movement on the uprised background that caused agap in the Eogene strata. With the W. Shandong region as a backbone, theNorth Shandong brush structural system incorpoxated the Neocathaysianfaults in its own, forming an concavo-convex brush structutre that convergedtowards southwest and diverged towards northeast. The Eogene sediments.were deposited in the sags, The W. Shandong backbone rotated counter-clo-ckwise with uplifing that resulted in the revival of the inner vortical layerof the W. Shandong brush stucture and the depositior of the Guanzuang'Formation in the sags. In E. Shandong, the Neocathaysian faults were deve--an lmportant role on the later gold enrichment in the gold ore deposits in:E. Shandong. The climate at that time was subtropical and there was a.notable difference of geomorphology in various parts of Shandong. In the.
rock salt and some sylvite, whereas in the Huangxian depression formedminable coal layers and in the Jiyang depression, an important oil field.(2 ) ln the Neogene, the tectonic activity along the Yishu fault zone.weakened and the North Shandong brush structural system subsided as a589.

〔590〕
whole. The Guantao and Minghuazhen formations of oil-bearing argillo-arenaceous rocks were deposited on the rises. The West Shandong brush structure was uplifting continuously, causing the absence of Mb. 1 and Mb.2 of the Shahejie Formation and the Dongying Formation; however, scatteredvolcanic lake and small lake sedimentation still occurred in the Linqu, Anqiuand Yishui districts. The E. Shandong uplifted area was nearly in a peneplainstate with senile river beds widely developed. Some of them, such as theTangshanpeng Conglomerate, bear alluvial gold deposits which were latercovered by the Pliocene basalt. Meanwhile, the Cathaysian structures weredeveloped in the southeast of Jiaodong peninsula where NE-trending lowermountains and hills were formed.ConclusionsThe monograph was compiled based on the results of the regional geological surveys (on the scale of 1:200,000) in Shandong which have beenalready finished and on the full use of other important data and achievements from the prospecting and exploration for mineral resources and from theanalysis of the data obtained according to the modern geological theory. Wehope that the research of the regional geology of Shandong will be advanced.

〔591〕
and problems are summarized.1. The stratigraphy of Shandong has been studied in a deep-going way.and the sound stratigraphic sequence and systems have been established.Secondly, the stratigraphic subregions have been classified and the strata.nomenclature unified. On the basis of the typical sections where the impo-rtant formations have been established, we discussed the subdivision and.correlation of the Taishan Group, the Jiaodong Group, the Jiapnan GrouP.the Fenzishan Group, the Wulian Group, the Cambrian, the Ordovician andthe Tertiary systems which have been generally subdivided into formationsbut a few to members, and occasionally only to groups. Some progress hasbeen made. For example, the Lower Proterozoic Fenzishan Group was dividedinto the lower subgroup of the Shanzhangjia and Mingcun fornations, andthe upper subgroup of the Zhujiakuang, Zhanggezhuang, Jutun, Gangyu and.collected that has prove that the Fenzishan Group is older than' the Cha-Igcheng Systern. The Uppre Proterozoic Tumen Formation has been restatedas the Tumen Group which includes five newly established formations, i. e.the Heishanguanzhuang-, Erqingshan-, Tongjiazhuang-, Fulaishan- and Shi--wangzhuang formations. The findings of stromatolite assemblages in the.Tongjiazhuang-, Fulaishan- and Shiwangzhuang formations and fifty-oneTumen Group can be correlated with the Qingbaikou System. The sequence:of the formations and members of the Upper Proterozoic Penglai Group.has been defined and from this group micropaleoplants of 36 species belon--ging to 23 genera have been found for the first time which could be grou-ped in time order into threeassemblages, among them three new genera.and seven new species have been identified. The first assemblage of plentymicroplants represented by Bavlinella has beed found in the black carbona-.ceous slates of the upper Baoshankou Formation and the stromatolite asse-this indicates that their host strata are stratigraphically higher than the.
the Xiadong area of China, as compared with the neighbouring areas. TheCambrian System in Shandong has been further defined and its classificationand correlation with that in other areas have been made by a biostra-tigraphic means. The system has been subdivided into eight formations:building sectionss in addition, the sedimentary facies. and environments ofthe Wushan-, Mantou-, Maozhuang-, Xuzhuang-, Zhangxia-, Gushan- and.

〔592〕
the formation-building sections have been also studied in more detail, as aresult the boundary between the lower and upper series has been definedand six fossil cephalopoda zones established, which provided a typical sectionof the Lower Palaeozoic in the North China stratigraphic region. The existence of the Triassic Liaocheng Formation has been confirmed while theprevious Jurassic Mengyin Group is believed to be invalid and its subformationsincluded inthe middle to lower Jurassic Wennan Formation based on some evidenceobtained. Detailed studies have also been made on the division and correlation of the Tertiary in the W. Shandong and North China stratigraphicsubregions and a comprehensive analysis of the fossils of vertebrates, mollusks, ostracoda, foraminifera, pisces, insects, algae, sporopollen, oncolites andpolychaeta worm tubes has been conducted, which is of great significance indetermining the age of the strata. Among them there are more importantvertebrates (comprising two fossil groups), gastropoda fauna (including threefossil groups), ostracoda fauna (including four fossil groups) and charophyteflora (comprising three fossil groups) of the Eogene and two significantbiotas of the Neogene, the Shanwang biota and the Pliocene Hipparionfauna. The Quaternary System has been studied in terms of genetic typesand sections of various sediments in Q1, Q2, Q3 and Q4 in plains and mountainous-hilly areas, and thus systematically generalized.2. In this monograph, we have classified the magmatic rocks in Shandong into the Taishanian, the early and late Taokeian, the early and lateYanshanian and the Himalayan stages, and discussed their distributions indifferent tectonic units. There is a general trend that the ages of magmaticrocks
distributed in the W. Shandong upwarped area formed mainlyin the Taishanian Stage, the early Taokeian Stage and the late YanshanianStage, whereas those developed in the E. Shandong upwarped area formedmainly in the early and late Taokeian Stage, the early and late YanshanianStage in particular; those in the late Yanshanian Stage are well developed.The magmatism in Shandong is featured by polyphase activities whichwere controlled by tectonism. This is especially true for the late Yanshanian granites which can be divided into the Aishan phase and the Laoshanphases, the former includes three pulses of intrusions and the latter includes five. As a result, complex bodies are usually developed in the W. Shandong upwarped area which are mainly controlled by NE- and EW-trendingregional faults, while composite masses are widely distributed in the E.

〔69〕
Shandong upwarped area which are chiefly controlled by NE- or NNE-tre-nding regional faults, On the other hand, the volcanic-intrusive complexes.are predominant in the Yishu fault zone which are basically controlled byNNE-trending regionai faults.In Shandong Province, the magmatic rocks have been evolved continuo-usly and irreversibly with respect to the lithology, rock-forming minerals,accessory mineral associations, petrochemistry, trace and rare earth elementsearly basic rocks to the later acid ones which is also reflected by the vari-ations in the amounts of major rock-forming minerals-potash feldspar andplagioclase- and the changes in the An content of plagioclase. There is aconsiderable difference between the pre-Yanshanian and the Yanshanianintrusive rocks: the former contain more plagioclase with higher An content(normally greater than 30) but less or very little potash-feldspar, whereasthe latter, especially granites, enrich the potash-feldspar content which isnearly equal to or more than the plagioclase content as the predominantphase in the rocks with the An content of plagioclase becoming less(often less than 30).So far as the composite granite mass is concerned, fromthe early inttusions to the later ones the amount of plagioclase and its Anyalues decrease.The magmatic rocks in Shandong Province have formed from variouspreliminarily into three genetic series, i. e. the mantle-derived series, theanatectic genetic series of mixed origin and the crust-transformed geneticseries, and some subseries. The predominant one is the anatectic geneticseries of mixed origin, or especially, the remelting genetic subseries of mixedorigin, and secondly, the crust-ttansformed genetic series (mainly distributedin W. Shandong). The three series, particularly the latte two, tend to evolvein parallel and are related to each other.3. In accordance with the data of petrology, petrochemistry, minera-Jogy and trace-element geochemistry, the establishment of metamorphic for-mations, the protolith restoration of metamorphic rocks, the classificationol metamorphic facies and facies series have been made for the Taishan-,Jiaodong-, Jiaonan-, Fenzishan-, Wulian-, Jining- and Penglai groups. TheTaishan Group is mainly of low amphibolite facies and partially of twopyroxenes grenulite faciest the Jiaodong Group,featured by prograde meta-morphism,is mainiy of arnphibolite-granulite subfacies plus high-- and lowanphibolite facies; the Jiaonan Group varies in metamorphic grade, whichis closely related to the stratigraphic sequence (horizon) and is mainly of

〔594〕
low amphibolite facies (Dashangou Fm.)-high greenschist facies (ZhenjiagouFm.)-low greenschist facies (Qiuguanzhuang Fm. and Yuialing Fm.), thelower Fenzishan Subgroup belongs to amphibolite-granulite subfacies otmedium-pressure series, and the upper subgroup, to low amphibolite faciesof medium-pressure seriess the Wulian Group- amphibolite facies of med-ium-low pressure seriest and finally the Penglai and Jining Groups--lowgreen-schist facies. In addition, four major metamorphic phases have beenidentified, that is, the Xintai (Taishan Gr.)-, the Jiaodong (Jiaodong Gr.and Jiaonan Gr.)-, the Fenzishanian (Fenzishan Gr., Wulian Gr.and Jining.Gr.)- and the Penglai <penglai Gr.) phases.A detailed study on the migmatization and its types for the rocks in.Shandong has beed carried out which reveals a feature that the older the.Archaean-Proterozoic strata are, the stronger its migmatization is. FheArchaean Taishan Group which has undergone intensive migmatization coversan alea about four-fifths of the cystalline basement, where eight migatiticfields with two migmatization phases have been defined according to thedistribution patterns of partial migmatites, migmatites and migmatitic gra-nites, The Late Archaean-Proterozoic Jiaodong and Jiaonan groups haveGroup, and contain usually partial migmatites in most areas, except for localanticlines or fault zones where occur some strongly migmatized rocks, suchas hamogenic migmatite. The lower subgroup of the Fenzishan Group is.similar to the Jiaodong Group in terms oi migmatization intensity while thechiefly of mechanical injections of felsic, quartz and pegmatitic veins. No-regional migmatization has been recognized in the upper Proterozoic PenglaiGroup.4. According to the basic principles and approaches of Li Siguang's.Geomechanics, the structures in Shandong Province can be classified into thelatitudinal structure systen, the regional EW-trending structures, the reg-ional NS-tending structures, the Neocathaysian System, the Cathaysian.structures, the West Shandong vortex structure, the North Shandong brushstructure and the East Shandong "a"-type structure. Meanwhile, the ancientbasement structures are roughly discussed and the generation, development,incorporation-combination relationship of various structural systems and theircontrol over rocks and ore-deposits are analyzed and elaborated.Moreover, the Yishu fault zone has been studied in a deep-going waywith its distribution, inner structures, dynamic features and geophysical cha-racteristics having been expounded in great detail and its forning history

〔599〕
also analyzed. In addition, the important views (with dispute up to now)and the research history on the fault zone are summarized. Some believed that it came into being in theArchaean or Proterozoic and moved for a long time; others considered it asa fault zone formed in the Mesozoic, which underwent mainly the left-handedstrike-slip movement(s), but there are still different opinions on the distance of its displacement.5. The geological development history of Shandong has been systematically described in terms of four evolutionary stages (the Archaean-Proterozoic, the Palaeozoic, the Mesozoic and the Cenozoic) on the basis of crustalmovements and geological processes. For the Archaean-Proterozoic stage, emphasis is put on the analyses of the geologic events and its evolution inShandong mainly according to the characteristics of deposition, magmatism,metamorphism, migmatization and tectonism as evidenced from the ArchaeanTaishan Group, the Lower Proterozoic Jining Group and the Upper Proterozoic Tumen Group of the Qingbaikou System in W. Shandong and fromthe Archaean-Proterozoic Jiaodong and Jiaonan groups, the Lower Proterozoic Fenzishan and Wulian groups and the Upper Proterozoic Penglai Groupin E. Shandong. For the Palaeozoic, when the crust in Shandong was relatively stable but with frequent oscillatory movements, discussion is mainlyfocused on the sedimentary facies and ancient environment changes. For theMeso-Cenozoic time when the crust in the region was in an activization state,the characteristics of sedimentation, magmatism, mineralization and formation and development of tectonic systems are discussed in accordance withthe Geomechanics.Thanks to the joint efforts made by the mass of geologists both within theprovince and beyond, a great deal of new progress and knowledge on the regional geology of Shandong has been achieved. However, it is worth while to notethat some problems remain to be further studied in the future, which includethose, for example, whether the supracrustal rocks below the Taishan Groupunderlie the Yanlingguan Formation or the Wanshanzhuang Formation; whether the Jurassic Fenshuiling and Laiyang formations should be assigned to theCretaceous or not; the gneiss and the age of the Linglong and Kunyushangranites; the relationship between the Jiaodong (or Jiaonan) Group andthe Fenzishan Group; the formation time and evolution of the Yishu faultzone, etc.Geological Publishing HouseBureau of Geology and MineralResources of Shandong ProvinceAddress: Hepingli, Beijing, China Address: Lishan Road 74,Ji'nan, China
